But I always get there.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself mentally replaying or repeating certain thoughts or actions related to your appearance when you're not actually in front of a mirror?\n\nPatient: Um... well, like I just mentioned... it's mostly just putting the lotion on, and... asking my coworkers if I look fine. I don't really know about mentally repeating things. I just... think about it before the workshops, I guess. That's really all.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it difficult to stop thinking about your appearance when you want to?\n\nPatient: I suppose so. It's... well, it's why I came in, really. Ever since I started helping out with the safety trainings at the plant, I just... I can't seem to stop noticing my hands. When I'm pointing things out to the newer workers, all I see is how wrinkly and uneven they look. And then... I start thinking about the newsletter photos they take. I keep thinking my face looks crooked in them. I try not to think about it, but... it's hard. Even when other people tell me they don't see what I'm talking about.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n8\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF45.2, F42\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I guess I'm here because... lately, I've been having some trouble at work. I work in production, and they asked me to start helping with the safety trainings. Which is fine, but... when I'm up there pointing things out, all I can see is how wrinkly and uneven my hands look. It's just... hard to ignore. And then they put some photos of the training in the company newsletter, and... my face just looks so crooked to me. I mentioned it to a couple of people, and they said they didn't see it, but... I don't know. It's just been bothering me a lot.\n\nDoctor: Do you notice your hands and face looking uneven or crooked when you're alone, or is it mostly when you're around other people?\n\nPatient: Um... I guess I notice it both times. It's... it's just there. I don't really know if it's worse one way or the other. I just see it.\n\nDoctor: Is your concern about your appearance affecting your ability to do your job or engage in social activities?\n\nPatient: Well... I still go to work. I still do my volunteer stuff. It hasn't stopped me from doing those things. But... I guess it takes me a bit longer to get ready in the mornings now, especially on training days. And... I try to stay out of the photos if I can. Sometimes I think twice before going out to things I used to just... go to. But I still go, mostly.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself repeating certain actions over and over, like checking your appearance in the mirror, in response to your concerns about your hands and face?\n\nPatient: Um... I guess I do check sometimes. Like, before a training class... or when I volunteer at the senior center workshops. I might go to the bathroom mirror a few times. Just... putting lotion on my hands, turning my face to see the different angles. Sometimes I ask a coworker if I look okay. It... it makes me run a little behind, I suppose. But I always get there.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself mentally replaying or repeating certain thoughts or actions related to your appearance when you're not actually in front of a mirror?\n\nPatient: Um... well, like I just mentioned... it's mostly just putting the lotion on, and... asking my coworkers if I look fine. I don't really know about mentally repeating things. I just... think about it before the workshops, I guess. That's really all.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it difficult to stop thinking about your appearance when you want to?\n\nPatient: I suppose so. It's... well, it's why I came in, really. Ever since I started helping out with the safety trainings at the plant, I just... I can't seem to stop noticing my hands. When I'm pointing things out to the newer workers, all I see is how wrinkly and uneven they look. And then... I start thinking about the newsletter photos they take. I keep thinking my face looks crooked in them. I try not to think about it, but... it's hard. Even when other people tell me they don't see what I'm talking about.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your concerns about your appearance are reasonable?\n\nPatient: I don't know. I mean... I know other people say they don't see it, but... I see it. So... I guess I think it makes sense that I'm worried about it. I'm not sure.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself thinking about your appearance when you don't want to?\n\nPatient: Well... yeah, I guess so. Like I said before... it's just hard to turn it off. I don't want to be thinking about my hands when I'm trying to do the safety trainings, but... I just can't help noticing the wrinkles and how uneven they look when I'm pointing at things. And... then my mind just goes to those newsletter photos and how crooked my face seems. Even when I try to focus on work, those thoughts just... they just kind of stay there. Even though people tell me I'm wrong.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I guess I'm here because... lately, I've been having some trouble at work. I work in production, and they asked me to start helping with the safety trainings. Which is fine, but... when I'm up there pointing things out, all I can see is how wrinkly and uneven my hands look. It's just... hard to ignore. And then they put some photos of the training in the company newsletter, and... my face just looks so crooked to me. I mentioned it to a couple of people, and they said they didn't see it, but... I don't know. It's just been bothering me a lot.\n\nDoctor: Do you notice your hands and face looking uneven or crooked when you're alone, or is it mostly when you're around other people?\n\nPatient: Um... I guess I notice it both times. It's... it's just there. I don't really know if it's worse one way or the other. I just see it.\n\nDoctor: Is your concern about your appearance affecting your ability to do your job or engage in social activities?\n\nPatient: Well... I still go to work. I still do my volunteer stuff. It hasn't stopped me from doing those things. But... I guess it takes me a bit longer to get ready in the mornings now, especially on training days. And... I try to stay out of the photos if I can. Sometimes I think twice before going out to things I used to just... go to. But I still go, mostly.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself repeating certain actions over and over, like checking your appearance in the mirror, in response to your concerns about your hands and face?\n\nPatient: Um... I guess I do check sometimes. Like, before a training class... or when I volunteer at the senior center workshops. I might go to the bathroom mirror a few times. But I always get there.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself mentally replaying or repeating certain thoughts or actions related to your appearance when you're not actually in front of a mirror?\n\nPatient: Um... well, like I just mentioned... it's mostly just putting the lotion on, and... asking my coworkers if I look fine. I don't really know about mentally repeating things. I just... think about it before the workshops, I guess. That's really all.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it difficult to stop thinking about your appearance when you want to?\n\nPatient: I suppose so. It's... well, it's why I came in, really. Ever since I started helping out with the safety trainings at the plant, I just... I can't seem to stop noticing my hands. When I'm pointing things out to the newer workers, all I see is how wrinkly and uneven they look. And then... I start thinking about the newsletter photos they take. I keep thinking my face looks crooked in them. I try not to think about it, but... it's hard. Even when other people tell me they don't see what I'm talking about.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your concerns about your appearance are reasonable?\n\nPatient: I don't know. I mean... I know other people say they don't see it, but... I see it. So... I guess I think it makes sense that I'm worried about it. I'm not sure.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself thinking about your appearance when you don't want to?\n\nPatient: Well... yeah, I guess so. Like I said before... it's just hard to turn it off. I don't want to be thinking about my hands when I'm trying to do the safety trainings, but... I just can't help noticing the wrinkles and how uneven they look when I'm pointing at things. And... then my mind just goes to those newsletter photos and how crooked my face seems. Even when I try to focus on work, those thoughts just... they just kind of stay there. Even though people tell me I'm wrong.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like thoughts about your appearance pop into your head without you wanting them to?\n\nPatient: Yes... I mean, like I was just saying. I don't ask for the thoughts to come, they just do... even when people tell me it's fine and they don't see it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're being forced to think about your appearance, even when you don't want to be?\n\nPatient: I don't know about... forced. From the dialogue so far and your current candidates, ask the one follow-up question that best separates them.\n\n[Current Turn]\n13\n\n[Current Candidates]\nF45.2, F42\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I guess I'm here because... lately, I've been having some trouble at work. I work in production, and they asked me to start helping with the safety trainings. Which is fine, but... when I'm up there pointing things out, all I can see is how wrinkly and uneven my hands look. It's just... hard to ignore. And then they put some photos of the training in the company newsletter, and... my face just looks so crooked to me. I mentioned it to a couple of people, and they said they didn't see it, but... I don't know. It's just been bothering me a lot.\n\nDoctor: Do you notice your hands and face looking uneven or crooked when you're alone, or is it mostly when you're around other people?\n\nPatient: Um... I guess I notice it both times. It's... it's just there. I don't really know if it's worse one way or the other. I just see it.\n\nDoctor: Is your concern about your appearance affecting your ability to do your job or engage in social activities?\n\nPatient: Well... I still go to work. I still do my volunteer stuff. It hasn't stopped me from doing those things. But... I guess it takes me a bit longer to get ready in the mornings now, especially on training days. And... I try to stay out of the photos if I can. Sometimes I think twice before going out to things I used to just... go to. But I still go, mostly.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself repeating certain actions over and over, like checking your appearance in the mirror, in response to your concerns about your hands and face?\n\nPatient: Um... I guess I do check sometimes. Like, before a training class... or when I volunteer at the senior center workshops. I might go to the bathroom mirror a few times. Just... putting lotion on my hands, turning my face to see the different angles. Sometimes I ask a coworker if I look okay. It... it makes me run a little behind, I suppose. But I always get there.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself mentally replaying or repeating certain thoughts or actions related to your appearance when you're not actually in front of a mirror?\n\nPatient: Um... well, like I just mentioned... it's mostly just putting the lotion on, and... asking my coworkers if I look fine. I don't really know about mentally repeating things. I just... think about it before the workshops, I guess. That's really all.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it difficult to stop thinking about your appearance when you want to?\n\nPatient: I suppose so. It's... well, it's why I came in, really. Ever since I started helping out with the safety trainings at the plant, I just... I can't seem to stop noticing my hands. When I'm pointing things out to the newer workers, all I see is how wrinkly and uneven they look. And then... I start thinking about the newsletter photos they take. I keep thinking my face looks crooked in them. I try not to think about it, but... it's hard. Even when other people tell me they don't see what I'm talking about.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your concerns about your appearance are reasonable?\n\nPatient: I don't know. I mean... I know other people say they don't see it, but... I see it. So... I guess I think it makes sense that I'm worried about it. I'm not sure.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself thinking about your appearance when you don't want to?\n\nPatient: Well... yeah, I guess so. Like I said before... it's just hard to turn it off. I don't want to be thinking about my hands when I'm trying to do the safety trainings, but... I just can't help noticing the wrinkles and how uneven they look when I'm pointing at things. And... then my mind just goes to those newsletter photos and how crooked my face seems. Even when I try to focus on work, those thoughts just... they just kind of stay there. Even though people tell me I'm wrong.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like thoughts about your appearance pop into your head without you wanting them to?\n\nPatient: Yes... I mean, like I was just saying. They just come up. I'll be pointing at a machine for the new folks, and suddenly I'm just staring at how wrinkly and uneven my hands are again. Or I'll just be sitting there and start worrying about my face looking crooked in those newsletter pictures. I don't ask for the thoughts to come, they just do... even when people tell me it's fine and they don't see it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're being forced to think about your appearance, even when you don't want to be?\n\nPatient: I don't know about... forced. That makes it sound like... I don't know. No one is making me do it. It's just... like I just said, the thoughts just kind of happen. I don't really know what else to call it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts about your appearance are unwanted?\n\nPatient: Well, yes. I... I don't want them there. Like I was saying, I'd rather just be able to focus on my job.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts about your appearance are intruding into your mind?\n\nPatient: I... I'm not really sure what you mean by intruding. I guess? Like I said, they just show up. I don't really have another way to explain it.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing.     "content": "You are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n14\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF45.2, F42\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I guess I'm here because... lately, I've been having some trouble at work. I work in production, and they asked me to start helping with the safety trainings. Which is fine, but... when I'm up there pointing things out, all I can see is how wrinkly and uneven my hands look. It's just... hard to ignore. And then they put some photos of the training in the company newsletter, and... my face just looks so crooked to me. I mentioned it to a couple of people, and they said they didn't see it, but... I don't know. It's just been bothering me a lot.\n\nDoctor: Do you notice your hands and face looking uneven or crooked when you're alone, or is it mostly when you're around other people?\n\nPatient: Um... I guess I notice it both times. It's... it's just there. I don't really know if it's worse one way or the other. I just see it.\n\nDoctor: Is your concern about your appearance affecting your ability to do your job or engage in social activities?\n\nPatient: Well... I still go to work. I still do my volunteer stuff. It hasn't stopped me from doing those things. But... I guess it takes me a bit longer to get ready in the mornings now, especially on training days. And... I try to stay out of the photos if I can. Sometimes I think twice before going out to things I used to just... go to. But I still go, mostly.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself repeating certain actions over and over, like checking your appearance in the mirror, in response to your concerns about your hands and face?\n\nPatient: Um... I guess I do check sometimes. Like, before a training class... or when I volunteer at the senior center workshops. I might go to the bathroom mirror a few times. Just... putting lotion on my hands, turning my face to see the different angles. Sometimes I ask a coworker if I look okay. It... it makes me run a little behind, I suppose. But I always get there.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself mentally replaying or repeating certain thoughts or actions related to your appearance when you're not actually in front of a mirror?\n\nPatient: Um... well, like I just mentioned... it's mostly just putting the lotion on, and... asking my coworkers if I look fine. I don't really know about mentally repeating things. I just... think about it before the workshops, I guess. That's really all.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it difficult to stop thinking about your appearance when you want to?\n\nPatient: I suppose so. It's... well, it's why I came in, really. Ever since I started helping out with the safety trainings at the plant, I just... I can't seem to stop noticing my hands. When I'm pointing things out to the newer workers, all I see is how wrinkly and uneven they look. And then... I start thinking about the newsletter photos they take. I keep thinking my face looks crooked in them. I try not to think about it, but... it's hard. Even when other people tell me they don't see what I'm talking about.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your concerns about your appearance are reasonable?\n\nPatient: I don't know. I mean... I know other people say they don't see it, but... I see it. So... I guess I think it makes sense that I'm worried about it. I'm not sure.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself thinking about your appearance when you don't want to?\n\nPatient: Well... yeah, I guess so. Like I said before... it's just hard to turn it off. I don't want to be thinking about my hands when I'm trying to do the safety trainings, but... I just can't help noticing the wrinkles and how uneven they look when I'm pointing at things. And... then my mind just goes to those newsletter photos and how crooked my face seems. Even when I try to focus on work, those thoughts just... they just kind of stay there. Even though people tell me I'm wrong.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like thoughts about your appearance pop into your head without you wanting them to?\n\nPatient: Yes... I mean, like I was just saying. They just come up. I'll be pointing at a machine for the new folks, and suddenly I'm just staring at how wrinkly and uneven my hands are again. Or I'll just be sitting there and start worrying about my face looking crooked in those newsletter pictures. I don't ask for the thoughts to come, they just do... even when people tell me it's fine and they don't see it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're being forced to think about your appearance, even when you don't want to be?\n\nPatient: I don't know about... forced. That makes it sound like... I don't know. No one is making me do it. It's just... like I just said, the thoughts just kind of happen. I don't really know what else to call it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts about your appearance are unwanted?\n\nPatient: Well, yes. I... I don't want them there. Like I was saying, I'd rather just be able to focus on my job.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts about your appearance are intruding into your mind?\n\nPatient: I... I'm not really sure what you mean by intruding. I guess? Like I said, they just show up. I don't really have another way to explain it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like thinking about your appearance is causing you a lot of distress?\n\nPatient: I wouldn't call it... distress. That sounds a bit much. It's just aggravating sometimes, I guess. But... I just deal with it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think people are judging you because of how you look?\n\nPatient: I don't know... not really. Folks at the plant are mostly just trying to get their work done. I don't think they're paying attention to me like that.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. That's really all.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it difficult to stop thinking about your appearance when you want to?\n\nPatient: I suppose so. It's... well, it's why I came in, really. Ever since I started helping out with the safety trainings at the plant, I just... I can't seem to stop noticing my hands. When I'm pointing things out to the newer workers, all I see is how wrinkly and uneven they look. And then... I start thinking about the newsletter photos they take. I keep thinking my face looks crooked in them. I try not to think about it, but... it's hard. Even when other people tell me they don't see what I'm talking about.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your concerns about your appearance are reasonable?\n\nPatient: I don't know. I mean... I know other people say they don't see it, but... I see it. So... I guess I think it makes sense that I'm worried about it. I'm not sure.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself thinking about your appearance when you don't want to?\n\nPatient: Well... yeah, I guess so. Like I said before... it's just hard to turn it off. I don't want to be thinking about my hands when I'm trying to do the safety trainings, but... I just can't help noticing the wrinkles and how uneven they look when I'm pointing at things. And... then my mind just goes to those newsletter photos and how crooked my face seems. Even when I try to focus on work, those thoughts just... they just kind of stay there. Even though people tell me I'm wrong.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like thoughts about your appearance pop into your head without you wanting them to?\n\nPatient: Yes... I mean, like I was just saying. They just come up. I'll be pointing at a machine for the new folks, and suddenly I'm just staring at how wrinkly and uneven my hands are again. Or I'll just be sitting there and start worrying about my face looking crooked in those newsletter pictures. I don't ask for the thoughts to come, they just do... even when people tell me it's fine and they don't see it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're being forced to think about your appearance, even when you don't want to be?\n\nPatient: I don't know about... forced. That makes it sound like... I don't know. No one is making me do it. It's just... like I just said, the thoughts just kind of happen. I don't really know what else to call it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts about your appearance are unwanted?\n\nPatient: Well, yes. I... I don't want them there. Like I was saying, I'd rather just be able to focus on my job.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts about your appearance are intruding into your mind?\n\nPatient: I... I'm not really sure what you mean by intruding. I guess? Like I said, they just show up. I don't really have another way to explain it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like thinking about your appearance is causing you a lot of distress?\n\nPatient: I wouldn't call it... distress. That sounds a bit much. It's just aggravating sometimes, I guess. But... I just deal with it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think people are judging you because of how you look?\n\nPatient: I don't know... not really. Folks at the plant are mostly just trying to get their work done. I don't think they're paying attention to me like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to perform certain rituals, such as checking your appearance, in a specific way or a certain number of times?\n\nPatient: I wouldn't call them rituals. That sounds a bit much. I just... well, before a class, or maybe a workshop at the senior center, I'll check the bathroom mirror a few times. \n\nI just put some lotion on my hands, turn my face different ways... just to check. Sometimes I might ask a coworker if I look all right. It makes me run a little behind sometimes, I guess, but... I still get myself there. It's not a big deal.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

I guess I notice it both times. It's... it's just there. I don't really know if it's worse one way or the other. I just see it.\n\nDoctor: Is your concern about your appearance affecting your ability to do your job or engage in social activities?\n\nPatient: Well... I still go to work. I still do my volunteer stuff. It hasn't stopped me from doing those things. But... I guess it takes me a bit longer to get ready in the mornings now, especially on training days. And... I try to stay out of the photos if I can. Sometimes I think twice before going out to things I used to just... go to. But I still go, mostly.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself repeating certain actions over and over, like checking your appearance in the mirror, in response to your concerns about your hands and face?\n\nPatient: Um... I guess I do check sometimes. Like, before a training class... or when I volunteer at the senior center workshops. I might go to the bathroom mirror a few times. Just... putting lotion on my hands, turning my face to see the different angles. Sometimes I ask a coworker if I look okay. It... it makes me run a little behind, I suppose. But I always get there.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself mentally replaying or repeating certain thoughts or actions related to your appearance when you're not actually in front of a mirror?\n\nPatient: Um... well, like I just mentioned... it's mostly just putting the lotion on, and... asking my coworkers if I look fine. I don't really know about mentally repeating things. I just... think about it before the workshops, I guess. That's really all.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it difficult to stop thinking about your appearance when you want to?\n\nPatient: I suppose so. It's... well, it's why I came in, really. Ever since I started helping out with the safety trainings at the plant, I just... I can't seem to stop noticing my hands. When I'm pointing things out to the newer workers, all I see is how wrinkly and uneven they look. And then... I start thinking about the newsletter photos they take. I keep thinking my face looks crooked in them. I try not to think about it, but... it's hard. Even when other people tell me they don't see what I'm talking about.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your concerns about your appearance are reasonable?\n\nPatient: I don't know. I mean... I know other people say they don't see it, but... I see it. So... I guess I think it makes sense that I'm worried about it. I'm not sure.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself thinking about your appearance when you don't want to?\n\nPatient: Well... yeah, I guess so. Like I said before... it's just hard to turn it off. I don't want to be thinking about my hands when I'm trying to do the safety trainings, but... I just can't help noticing the wrinkles and how uneven they look when I'm pointing at things. And... then my mind just goes to those newsletter photos and how crooked my face seems. Even when I try to focus on work, those thoughts just... they just kind of stay there. Even though people tell me I'm wrong.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like thoughts about your appearance pop into your head without you wanting them to?\n\nPatient: Yes... I mean, like I was just saying. They just come up. I'll be pointing at a machine for the new folks, and suddenly I'm just staring at how wrinkly and uneven my hands are again. Or I'll just be sitting there and start worrying about my face looking crooked in those newsletter pictures. I don't ask for the thoughts to come, they just do... even when people tell me it's fine and they don't see it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're being forced to think about your appearance, even when you don't want to be?\n\nPatient: I don't know about... forced. That makes it sound like... I don't know. No one is making me do it. It's just... like I just said, the thoughts just kind of happen. I don't really know what else to call it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts about your appearance are unwanted?\n\nPatient: Well, yes. I... I don't want them there. Like I was saying, I'd rather just be able to focus on my job.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts about your appearance are intruding into your mind?\n\nPatient: I... I'm not really sure what you mean by intruding. I guess? Like I said, they just show up. I don't really have another way to explain it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like thinking about your appearance is causing you a lot of distress?\n\nPatient: I wouldn't call it... distress. That sounds a bit much. It's just aggravating sometimes, I guess. But... I just deal with it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think people are judging you because of how you look?\n\nPatient: I don't know... not really. Folks at the plant are mostly just trying to get their work done. I don't think they're paying attention to me like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to perform certain rituals, such as checking your appearance, in a specific way or a certain number of times?\n\nPatient: I wouldn't call them rituals. That sounds a bit much. I just... well, before a class, or maybe a workshop at the senior center, I'll check the bathroom mirror a few times. \n\nI just put some lotion on my hands, turn my face different ways... just to check. Sometimes I might ask a coworker if I look all right. It makes me run a little behind sometimes, I guess, but... I still get myself there. It's not a big deal.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your appearance would be less of a concern for you if you didn't have to be in front of other people at work or in social situations?\n\nPatient: I don't really know. I haven't thought about it like that. I suppose I'd still notice things... even if I was just at home by myself. It's just how I am, I guess.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I guess I'm here because... lately, I've been having some trouble at work. I work in production, and they asked me to start helping with the safety trainings. Which is fine, but... when I'm up there pointing things out, all I can see is how wrinkly and uneven my hands look. It's just... hard to ignore. And then they put some photos of the training in the company newsletter, and... my face just looks so crooked to me. I mentioned it to a couple of people, and they said they didn't see it, but... I don't know. It's just been bothering me a lot.\n\nDoctor: Do you notice your hands and face looking uneven or crooked when you're alone, or is it mostly when you're around other people?\n\nPatient: Um... I guess I notice it both times. It's... it's just there. I don't really know if it's worse one way or the other. I just see it.\n\nDoctor: Is your concern about your appearance affecting your ability to do your job or engage in social activities?\n\nPatient: Well... I still go to work. I still do my volunteer stuff. It hasn't stopped me from doing those things. But... I guess it takes me a bit longer to get ready in the mornings now, especially on training days. And... I try to stay out of the photos if I can. Sometimes I think twice before going out to things I used to just... go to. But I still go, mostly.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself repeating certain actions over and over, like checking your appearance in the mirror, in response to your concerns about your hands and face?\n\nPatient: Um... I guess I do check sometimes. Like, before a training class... or when I volunteer at the senior center workshops. I might go to the bathroom mirror a few times. Just... putting lotion on my hands, turning my face to see the different angles. Sometimes I ask a coworker if I look okay. It... it makes me run a little behind, I suppose. But I always get there.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself mentally replaying or repeating certain thoughts or actions related to your appearance when you're not actually in front of a mirror?\n\nPatient: Um... well, like I just mentioned... it's mostly just putting the lotion on, and... asking my coworkers if I look fine. I don't really know about mentally repeating things. I just... think about it before the workshops, I guess. That's really all.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it difficult to stop thinking about your appearance when you want to?\n\nPatient: I suppose so. It's... well, it's why I came in, really. Ever since I started helping out with the safety trainings at the plant, I just... I can't seem to stop noticing my hands. When I'm pointing things out to the newer workers, all I see is how wrinkly and uneven they look. And then... I start thinking about the newsletter photos they take. I keep thinking my face looks crooked in them. I try not to think about it, but... it's hard. Even when other people tell me they don't see what I'm talking about.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your concerns about your appearance are reasonable?\n\nPatient: I don't know. I mean... I know other people say they don't see it, but... I see it. So... I guess I think it makes sense that I'm worried about it. I'm not sure.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself thinking about your appearance when you don't want to?\n\nPatient: Well... yeah, I guess so. Like I said before... it's just hard to turn it off. I don't want to be thinking about my hands when I'm trying to do the safety trainings, but... I just can't help noticing the wrinkles and how uneven they look when I'm pointing at things. And... then my mind just goes to those newsletter photos and how crooked my face seems. Even when I try to focus on work, those thoughts just... they just kind of stay there. Even though people tell me I'm wrong.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like thoughts about your appearance pop into your head without you wanting them to?\n\nPatient: Yes... I mean, like I was just saying. They just come up. I'll be pointing at a machine for the new folks, and suddenly I'm just staring at how wrinkly and uneven my hands are again. Or I'll just be sitting there and start worrying about my face looking crooked in those newsletter pictures. I don't ask for the thoughts to come, they just do... even when people tell me it's fine and they don't see it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're being forced to think about your appearance, even when you don't want to be?\n\nPatient: I don't know about... forced. That makes it sound like... I don't know. No one is making me do it. It's just... like I just said, the thoughts just kind of happen. I don't really know what else to call it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts about your appearance are unwanted?\n\nPatient: Well, yes. I... I don't want them there. Like I was saying, I'd rather just be able to focus on my job.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts about your appearance are intruding into your mind?\n\nPatient: I... I'm not really sure what you mean by intruding. I guess? Like I said, they just show up. I don't really have another way to explain it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like thinking about your appearance is causing you a lot of distress?\n\nPatient: I wouldn't call it... distress. That sounds a bit much. It's just aggravating sometimes, I guess. But... I just deal with it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think people are judging you because of how you look?\n\nPatient: I don't know... not really. Folks at the plant are mostly just trying to get their work done. I don't think they're paying attention to me like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to perform certain rituals, such as checking your appearance, in a specific way or a certain number of times?\n\nPatient: I wouldn't call them rituals. That sounds a bit much. I just... well, before a class, or maybe a workshop at the senior center, I'll check the bathroom mirror a few times. \n\nI just put some lotion on my hands, turn my face different ways... just to check. It's just... hard to ignore. And then they put some photos of the training in the company newsletter, and... my face just looks so crooked to me. I mentioned it to a couple of people, and they said they didn't see it, but... I don't know. It's just been bothering me a lot.\n\nDoctor: Do you notice your hands and face looking uneven or crooked when you're alone, or is it mostly when you're around other people?\n\nPatient: Um... I guess I notice it both times. It's... it's just there. I don't really know if it's worse one way or the other. I just see it.\n\nDoctor: Is your concern about your appearance affecting your ability to do your job or engage in social activities?\n\nPatient: Well... I still go to work. I still do my volunteer stuff. It hasn't stopped me from doing those things. But... I guess it takes me a bit longer to get ready in the mornings now, especially on training days. And... I try to stay out of the photos if I can. Sometimes I think twice before going out to things I used to just... go to. But I still go, mostly.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself repeating certain actions over and over, like checking your appearance in the mirror, in response to your concerns about your hands and face?\n\nPatient: Um... I guess I do check sometimes. Like, before a training class... or when I volunteer at the senior center workshops. I might go to the bathroom mirror a few times. Just... putting lotion on my hands, turning my face to see the different angles. Sometimes I ask a coworker if I look okay. It... it makes me run a little behind, I suppose. But I always get there.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself mentally replaying or repeating certain thoughts or actions related to your appearance when you're not actually in front of a mirror?\n\nPatient: Um... well, like I just mentioned... it's mostly just putting the lotion on, and... asking my coworkers if I look fine. I don't really know about mentally repeating things. I just... think about it before the workshops, I guess. That's really all.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it difficult to stop thinking about your appearance when you want to?\n\nPatient: I suppose so. It's... well, it's why I came in, really. Ever since I started helping out with the safety trainings at the plant, I just... I can't seem to stop noticing my hands. When I'm pointing things out to the newer workers, all I see is how wrinkly and uneven they look. And then... I start thinking about the newsletter photos they take. I keep thinking my face looks crooked in them. I try not to think about it, but... it's hard. Even when other people tell me they don't see what I'm talking about.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your concerns about your appearance are reasonable?\n\nPatient: I don't know. I mean... I know other people say they don't see it, but... I see it. So... I guess I think it makes sense that I'm worried about it. I'm not sure.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself thinking about your appearance when you don't want to?\n\nPatient: Well... yeah, I guess so. Like I said before... it's just hard to turn it off. I don't want to be thinking about my hands when I'm trying to do the safety trainings, but... I just can't help noticing the wrinkles and how uneven they look when I'm pointing at things. And... then my mind just goes to those newsletter photos and how crooked my face seems. Even when I try to focus on work, those thoughts just... they just kind of stay there. Even though people tell me I'm wrong.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like thoughts about your appearance pop into your head without you wanting them to?\n\nPatient: Yes... I mean, like I was just saying. They just come up. I'll be pointing at a machine for the new folks, and suddenly I'm just staring at how wrinkly and uneven my hands are again. I don't ask for the thoughts to come, they just do... even when people tell me it's fine and they don't see it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're being forced to think about your appearance, even when you don't want to be?\n\nPatient: I don't know about... forced. It's just... hard to ignore. And then they put some photos of the training in the company newsletter, and... my face just looks so crooked to me. I mentioned it to a couple of people, and they said they didn't see it, but... I don't know. It's just been bothering me a lot.\n\nDoctor: Do you notice your hands and face looking uneven or crooked when you're alone, or is it mostly when you're around other people?\n\nPatient: Um... I guess I notice it both times. It's... it's just there. I don't really know if it's worse one way or the other. I just see it.\n\nDoctor: Is your concern about your appearance affecting your ability to do your job or engage in social activities?\n\nPatient: Well... I still go to work. I still do my volunteer stuff. It hasn't stopped me from doing those things. But... I guess it takes me a bit longer to get ready in the mornings now, especially on training days. And... I try to stay out of the photos if I can. Sometimes I think twice before going out to things I used to just... go to. But I still go, mostly.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself repeating certain actions over and over, like checking your appearance in the mirror, in response to your concerns about your hands and face?\n\nPatient: Um... I guess I do check sometimes. Like, before a training class... or when I volunteer at the senior center workshops. I might go to the bathroom mirror a few times. But I always get there.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself mentally replaying or repeating certain thoughts or actions related to your appearance when you're not actually in front of a mirror?\n\nPatient: Um... well, like I just mentioned... it's mostly just putting the lotion on, and... asking my coworkers if I look fine. I don't really know about mentally repeating things. I just... think about it before the workshops, I guess. That's really all.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it difficult to stop thinking about your appearance when you want to?\n\nPatient: I suppose so. It's... well, it's why I came in, really. Ever since I started helping out with the safety trainings at the plant, I just... I can't seem to stop noticing my hands. When I'm pointing things out to the newer workers, all I see is how wrinkly and uneven they look. And then... I start thinking about the newsletter photos they take. I keep thinking my face looks crooked in them. I try not to think about it, but... it's hard. Even when other people tell me they don't see what I'm talking about.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your concerns about your appearance are reasonable?\n\nPatient: I don't know. I mean... I know other people say they don't see it, but... I see it. So... I guess I think it makes sense that I'm worried about it. I'm not sure.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself thinking about your appearance when you don't want to?\n\nPatient: Well... yeah, I guess so. Like I said before... it's just hard to turn it off. I don't want to be thinking about my hands when I'm trying to do the safety trainings, but... I just can't help noticing the wrinkles and how uneven they look when I'm pointing at things. And... then my mind just goes to those newsletter photos and how crooked my face seems. Even when I try to focus on work, those thoughts just... they just kind of stay there. Even though people tell me I'm wrong.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like thoughts about your appearance pop into your head without you wanting them to?\n\nPatient: Yes... I mean, like I was just saying. They just come up. I'll be pointing at a machine for the new folks, and suddenly I'm just staring at how wrinkly and uneven my hands are again. Or I'll just be sitting there and start worrying about my face looking crooked in those newsletter pictures. I don't ask for the thoughts to come, they just do... even when people tell me it's fine and they don't see it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're being forced to think about your appearance, even when you don't want to be?\n\nPatient: I don't know about... forced. That makes it sound like... I don't know. No one is making me do it. It's just... like I just said, the thoughts just kind of happen. I'm not really sure what you mean by intruding. I guess? Like I said, they just show up. I don't really have another way to explain it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like thinking about your appearance is causing you a lot of distress?\n\nPatient: I wouldn't call it... distress. That sounds a bit much. It's just aggravating sometimes, I guess. But... I just deal with it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think people are judging you because of how you look?\n\nPatient: I don't know... not really. Folks at the plant are mostly just trying to get their work done. I don't think they're paying attention to me like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to perform certain rituals, such as checking your appearance, in a specific way or a certain number of times?\n\nPatient: I wouldn't call them rituals. That sounds a bit much. It's not a big deal.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your appearance would be less of a concern for you if you didn't have to be in front of other people at work or in social situations?\n\nPatient: I don't really know. I haven't thought about it like that. I suppose I'd still notice things... even if I was just at home by myself. It's just how I am, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still worry about your appearance if everyone around you told you that you look fine and there's nothing wrong with how you look?\n\nPatient: I... I suppose so. Even if they said that, it's... it's hard to just turn off how you see yourself. I'd probably still think about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your concerns about your appearance are a part of who you are?\n\nPatient: I don't really know. I guess I don't look at it that way. I haven't really sat down and thought about if it's... my identity or anything like that. It's just something on my mind sometimes. I just go to work and do my thing.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still worry about your appearance if you were certain that no one was judging you?\n\nPatient: I don't really know. It's... hard to say. I guess I'd still see the same things when I look in the mirror, so... probably. But I'm not sure.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you cannot control your thoughts about your appearance?\n\nPatient: I don't really know about that. I mean... I wouldn't say I can't control them. I just go about my day.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out.
